and the homogeneity of
P_{\alpha}
and of
\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}
we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(x\delta_{r}(z))-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(z)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(x\delta_{r}(z))-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(\delta_{r}(z)))P_{\alpha}(z)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr.

In order to obtain the last equality, we have exploited the equality

(66)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}D_{h}\phi(x)(y)P_{\alpha}(y)dy=0,

that is justified by the combination of few results. In fact, first we know that
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset C^{1}(\mathbb{G})\subset C^{1}_{h}(\mathbb{G})
, then by the definition of h-homomorphism we know that for every
x,y\in\mathbb{G}
it holds that

D_{h}\phi(x)(y)=\langle\nabla_{H}\phi(x),y\rangle=-\langle\nabla_{H}\phi(x),-y\rangle=-\langle\nabla_{H}\phi(x),y^{-1}\rangle=-D_{h}\phi(x)(y^{-1}).

Thus (
66
) follows by the following remarks

•

by the homogeneity of
d_{c}
, if
y\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
, then
y^{-1}\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
.

•

by the property of the heat kernel
h
stated in Theorem
2.12
(iii), it can be verified that

\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}=\|y^{-1}\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}

for every
y\in\mathbb{G}
,
y\neq 0
.
Hence, also
P_{\alpha}(y)=P_{\alpha}(y^{-1})
holds.

Now, by Theorem
2.9
(in light of (
9
) and (
10
)), we know that

|\phi(x\delta_{r}(z))-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(\delta_{r}(z)))|=O(r^{2})\quad\mathrm{\ as\ }r\to 0,

as a consequence we know that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\phi(x)||P_{\alpha}(y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha+1}dr\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|P_{\alpha}(z)|d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=c\ \frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\sigma(\alpha),

for some suitable constant
c>0
. The integral converges for every
\alpha
such that
\alpha>-2
, since

\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\sigma(\alpha)=\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|\alpha}\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+2}\sigma(\alpha)

and

\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})\alpha=2
.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

For every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
and for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
the map

(67)

\alpha\to\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

is analytic on
(-2,Q)
.

Proof.

Let us consider the
k
-th derivative of (
67
) with respect to
\alpha
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular, we will show that derivative and integral can be exchanged by estimating the term

(68)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|.

We split the integral of (
67
) as

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|\leq

(69)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|.

The second term can be dealt with repeating the steps in (
3.1
)
and we get that

(70)

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\ln^{k}(t)h(t,y)dtdy\right|\\
&\leq\sup_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(x\cdot)-\phi(x)|\sup_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}h(1,\cdot)\mu(\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\frac{2}{(Q-\alpha)^{k+1}}k!.\end{split}

Then we need to deal with

\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|.

By exploiting the equality (
66
) justified in Lemma
3.9
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(y))\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|

(71)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(y)|\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(y)|h(t,y)dydt.

Now, by Theorem
2.9
(in light of (
9
) and (
10
)), we know that

|\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(y))|=O(\|y\|_{c}^{2})\quad\mathrm{\ as\ }\|y\|_{c}\to 0.

Hence, for some
C\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(y)|h(t,y)dydt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{c}^{2}h(t,y)dydt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}t^{-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{c}^{2}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y))dydt,

due to the homogeneity of
h
. Now, by a change of variables
z=\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y)
and by the homogeneity of the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{G}
we get

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}t^{-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{c}^{2}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y))dydt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}})}\|\delta_{\sqrt{t}}(z)\|_{c}^{2}h(1,z)dzdt

(72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}})}t\|z\|_{c}^{2}h(1,z)dzdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}dt\int_{\mathbb{G}}\|z\|_{c}^{2}h(1,z)dz.

Notice that
\int_{\mathbb{G}}\|z\|_{c}^{2}h(1,z)dz<\infty
since
h(1,z)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
. Now, observe that, according to (
32
),

b_{k}=\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}dt,

when
N=1
, recalling that
N=1>-\frac{\alpha}{2}
if
\alpha>-2
. Hence, we can repeat the argument involving the estimate in (
33
) obtaining that for every
k\in\mathbb{N}

(73)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}dt\leq C\frac{2}{(\alpha+2)^{k+1}}k!.\end{split}

By combining (
70
), (
3.3
), (
3.3
) and (
73
) the proof is concluded.
∎

Now, we are in position to prove Theorem
3.8
.

Proof of Theorem
3.8
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
. We rearrange the map
(0,Q)\ni\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
as

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

(74)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(x)P_{\alpha}(y)dy.

Since
\alpha\in(0,Q)
, we can compute the third integral of the sum in (
3.3
) exploiting the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
and homogeneity properties, getting

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(x)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}\|\delta_{r}(z)\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(\delta_{r}(z))dr

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}r^{\alpha-Q}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}r^{Q-1}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(z)dr

(75)

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(z)dr

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\sigma(\alpha)\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-1}dr\sigma(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\sigma(\alpha)\lim_{a\to 0}\bigg[\frac{r^{\alpha}}{\alpha}\bigg]_{r=a}^{1}\sigma(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha).

Hence, for every
\alpha\in(0,Q)
the equality

(76)

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

holds.

By Remark
3.5
, the first term of the sum in (
76
) is analytic with respect to
\alpha
on
(-\infty,Q)
.
On the other hand, the third term of the sum in (
76
) is analytic with respect to
\alpha
on
(-\infty,Q)
, since by Item
2
in Section
2.5
,
\alpha\to\sigma(\alpha)
is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
and
\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})\alpha=2
.

We point out that by Lemma
3.9
, the expression in (
76
) is well-defined for every
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
. Eventually, by Lemma
3.10
, the second term of the sum in (
76
) is analytic with respect to
\alpha
on
(-2,Q)
and this concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

Let us show that
\psi(x,0)=\phi(x)
.
Since

(1)

\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\frac{1}{\alpha\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}=\frac{1}{2},

(2)

by Item
3
of Section
2.5
,
\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\sigma(\alpha)=2,

then

\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\frac{1}{\alpha\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\sigma(\alpha)=1

holds. Since
\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}=0,
then
\psi(x,0)=\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\psi(x,\alpha)
is

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to 0}\left(\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy+\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)\right)=\phi(x).

In the following proposition, we deduce an useful representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
valid for
\alpha\in(-2,0)
.

Proposition 3.12
.

For every
x\in\mathbb{G}
, for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
the following representation holds

(77)

\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{G}}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy,

for every
\alpha\in(-2,0)
.

Proof.

Let us compute for
-2<\alpha<0
the following integral

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(x)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

(78)

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}\|\delta_{r}(z)\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(\delta_{r}(z))dr

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\lim_{a\to\infty}\bigg[\frac{r^{\alpha}}{\alpha}\bigg]_{r=1}^{a}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(z)

\displaystyle=-\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha).

As usual, we have exploited the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
and the homogeneity properties. The proof follows by combining Theorem
3.8
and (
3.3
).
∎

Remark 3.13
.

The results of the current subsection hold as well being true if
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
and
|\phi(z)|\leq K\|z\|_{c}^{-L}
as
\|z\|_{c}>S
for some
L,S,K>0
. In particular, under these hypotheses one can check that
\psi(x,\alpha)
can be analytically continued on the interval
(-2,\min\{Q,L\})
.

3.4.
Extension of the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{s}
to
-\frac{Q}{2}<s<1

In this section, we introduce a further notation. For every fixed
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
it is useful to denote the map
\psi(x,\alpha)
also by
\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
. This notation appears in the paper only when we wish to emphasize the dependence of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on the choice of the map
\phi
.
By combining
[
11
, Theorem 3.11]
, which states the pointwise integral representation of
\mathcal{L}^{s}\phi(x)
in (
26
), and the representation (
77
) of
\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
showed in Proposition
3.12
, we notice that for every
s\in(0,1)
,
x\in\mathbb{G}
, and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
the equality

(79)

\psi_{\phi}(x,-2s)=\mathcal{L}^{s}\phi(x)

holds. Since for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
we have analytically continued the map
\alpha\to\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
on
(-2,Q)
, we can introduce for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
a family of tempered distributions
\{\widetilde{P}_{\alpha}^{x}\}_{\alpha\in(-2,Q)}
by re-reading the map
\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
as a the action of
\widetilde{P}^{x}_{\alpha}
on
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
, so that for every
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
we have

\widetilde{P}_{\alpha}^{x}(\phi):=\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha).

Thus, for every
s\in(0,1)
and
x\in\mathbb{G}
we have

\widetilde{P}_{-2s}^{x}(\phi)=\psi_{\phi}(x,-2s)=\mathcal{L}^{s}\phi(x),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
.
We notice that, when
0<\alpha<Q
,
P_{\alpha}
is a distribution as well and
\widetilde{P}^{x}_{\alpha}
and
P_{\alpha}
satisfy the following relation

(80)

\widetilde{P}_{\alpha}^{x}(\phi)=P_{\alpha}(\phi\circ l_{x})=\int_{\mathbb{G}}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy,

for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
. It is natural to introduce the following notion.

Definition 3.14
.

For every
s\in(-\frac{Q}{2},1)
,

\mathcal{L}^{s}\phi(x):=\psi_{\phi}(x,-2s)=\widetilde{P}_{-2s}^{x}(\phi),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
and for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
.

In the following two subsections we study the properties of the operator
\mathcal{L}^{s}
introduced in Definition
3.14
.

3.5.
An estimate of
x\to\psi(x,\alpha)

First, we observe that
\mathbb{G}\ni x\to\psi(x,\alpha)
is a smooth map. With the following result, later we wish to prove the consistence of the extension of the operator
\mathcal{L}^{s}
provided by Definition
3.14
.

Proposition 3.15
.

For every
\alpha>0
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})

\psi(x,\alpha)=O(\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q})

as
\|x\|_{c}\to\infty
.

Proof.

Recalling the estimate in (
25
) and the fact that
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy=\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\phi(xy)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy\leq\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{G}}|\phi(xy)|\|y\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{G}}|\phi(z)|\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}dz=\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{G}}|\phi(z)|\frac{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}dz=\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz+\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz,

where
C_{\alpha}
and
c_{k}
are suitable positive constants.
Let us discuss separately the two terms of the last line. By exploiting homogeneity properties and the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}

\displaystyle C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dy\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{{2}^{Q-\alpha}}{\|x\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}dy

(81)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{{2}^{Q-\alpha}}{\|x\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{{2}^{Q-\alpha}}{\|x\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+r^{k})}r^{Q-1}dy.

The integral converges for every
k>Q
. Hence, we proved that for every
k>Q
the estimate

(82)

\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dy\leq c(k,\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}

holds for some suitable positive constant
c(k,\alpha)
.

Concerning the second term of the sum, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|xx^{-1}z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+(\|x\|_{c}-\|x^{-1}z\|_{c})^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{1}{(1+(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})^{k})}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{1}{(1+(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})^{k})}\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\int_{0}^{\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2}}r^{\alpha-Q}r^{Q-1}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{1}{(1+(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})^{k})}\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\frac{(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})^{\alpha}}{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}2^{k}\|x\|_{c}^{-k}\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\frac{\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha}}{2^{\alpha}\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}(k,\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-k},

where
\tilde{c}(k,\alpha)
is a suitable positive constant. Hence, we have proved that for every
k
we have

(83)

\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}C_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz\leq\tilde{c}(k,\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-k}.

By combining (
82
) and (
83
), we get that for every
k>Q
the estimate

\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\phi(xy)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy\leq c(k,\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}+\tilde{c}(k,\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-k}\leq C(\alpha,k)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}

holds, where
C(k,\alpha)
is a suitable positive constant, concluding the proof.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

For every
-2<\alpha<0
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})

(84)

\psi(x,\alpha)=O(\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q})

as
\|x\|_{c}\to\infty
.

Proof.

We can use the representation in (
77
), i.e. for
\alpha\in(-2,0)

\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{G}}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy.

Let
b=b(\mathbb{G})>0
the constant which appears in Theorem
2.9
and consider

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy

(85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy-\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(x)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy.

We deal with the three terms of the sum in (
3.5
), separately. Let us consider the first one, in particular

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(xy)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(z)\|x^{-1}z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(z)\frac{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\|x^{-1}z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}c_{k}C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz,

holds for some positive constant
C_{\alpha}
.
Now, as in (
3.5
) (since
\alpha<Q
), we get the estimate in (
82
), i.e. for every
k>Q
the estimate

(86)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy\leq\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}c_{k}c_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\frac{1}{(1+\|z\|_{c}^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha}}dz\leq c(k,\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}\end{split}

holds.
Let us consider the second term of (
3.5
). By the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
and homogeneity properties we obtain

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(x)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=-\phi(x)\int_{\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}}}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=-\phi(x)\int_{\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}}}^{\infty}r^{\alpha-Q}r^{Q-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}P_{\alpha}(z)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)\frac{\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha}}{(2b^{2})^{\alpha}}.

We point out that we used that
\alpha\in(-2,0)
. Now, since
\phi(x)=O(\|x\|_{c}^{N})
as
\|x\|_{c}\to\infty
for every negative integer
N
, fixing
N=-Q
we get that for
\|x\|_{c}>1
the estimate

(87)

\begin{split}-\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\phi(x)P_{\alpha}(y)dy\leq\widehat{c}(\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q}\phi(x)\end{split}

holds. By a change of variables in the third term of the sum in (
3.5
), we obtain

(88)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)dy=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(z)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(x^{-1}z)dz.\end{split}

Recalling the stratified mean value inequality of Theorem
2.9
and the fact that
\phi
is smooth, we have the following inequality

(89)

|\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(y)|=|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{1}(\phi,x)(xy)|\leq c\|y\|_{c}^{2}\sup_{p\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,b^{2}\|y\|_{c})}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi(p),

where
\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi
is
\mathfrak{d}_{h}^{2}\phi(z):\mathbb{G}\to\mathbb{R},z\to\max_{|\alpha|=2}|(Z_{1}\ldots Z_{m_{1}})^{\alpha}\phi(z)|
for every
z\in\mathbb{G}
. We get

(90)

|\phi(z)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(x^{-1}z)|\leq c\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{2}\sup_{p\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,b^{2}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c})}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi(p)=c\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi(\xi),

for some
\xi=\xi(z)\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,b^{2}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c})\subset\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})
.
Now, considering the Pansu differential
D_{h}\phi(x)
of
\phi
at
x
, we notice that the following equality

(91)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}D_{h}\phi(x)(x^{-1}z)P_{\alpha}(x^{-1}z)dz=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}D_{h}\phi(x)(y)P_{\alpha}(y)dy=0\end{split}

holds, by the same argument in (
66
).
Hence, from (
88
), exploiting (
25
), (
90
) and (
91
) and the fact that
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(z)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(x^{-1}z)dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(z)-\phi(x)-D_{h}\phi(x)(x^{-1}z))P_{\alpha}(x^{-1}z)dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}c\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi(\xi)|P_{\alpha}(x^{-1}z)|dz

(92)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi(\xi)\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q+2}dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{h}\phi(\xi)\frac{(1+\|\xi\|_{c}^{k})}{(1+\|\xi\|_{c}^{k})}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q+2}dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\frac{1}{(1+\|\xi\|_{c}^{k})}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q+2}dz,

where
c
and
c_{\alpha}
are suitable positive constants.
Now, remarking that

\|\xi\|_{c}\geq\|x\|_{c}-\|x^{-1}\xi\|_{c}\geq\|x\|_{c}-b^{2}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}\geq\|x\|_{c}-b^{2}\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}}=\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2},

we can continue from (
3.5
) getting

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}(\phi(z)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(x^{-1}z)dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\frac{1}{(1+\|\xi\|_{c}^{k})}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q+2}dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}c_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2}})}\frac{1}{(1+\big(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2}\big)^{k})}\frac{1}{\|x^{-1}z\|_{c}^{Q-\alpha-2}}dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{1}{(1+\big(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2}\big)^{k})}\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\int_{0}^{\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2}}r^{\alpha+2-Q+Q-1}dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}cC_{\alpha}c_{k}\frac{1}{(1+\big(\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2}\big)^{k})}\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\frac{\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha+2}}{2^{\alpha+2}(\alpha+2)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ c(\alpha,k)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha+2-k},

where
c(\alpha,k)
is a suitable positive constant.

Thus, by choosing
k>Q+2
we get the following estimate

(93)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})}(\phi(xy)-\phi(x))P_{\alpha}(y)\leq\widetilde{c}(\alpha)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q},\end{split}

for a suitable constant
\widetilde{c}(\alpha)
.
Combining (
86
), (
87
) and (
93
) we obtain (
84
).
∎

3.6.
Some properties of
\mathcal{L}^{s}
, when
-\frac{Q}{2}<s<1

Coherently with the continuation constructed in the previous subsections, we show that the definition of the fractional sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}^{s}
with argument
s\in(-\frac{Q}{2},1)
, that we introduced in Definition
3.14
is consistent.

First, we observe that for
s=0
, it holds that

\psi_{\phi}(x,0)=\phi(x)=\mathcal{L}^{0}\phi(x),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
and
x\in\mathbb{G}
.

Moreover,
\mathcal{L}^{s}
satisfies the semigroup property stated in the following proposition.

Proposition 3.17
.

For every
s,m\in(-\frac{Q}{2},1)
such that
-\frac{Q}{2}<m+s<1
the equality

\mathcal{L}^{s}\circ\mathcal{L}^{m}=\mathcal{L}^{s+m}

holds.

Proof.

Let us consider
\alpha\in(0,Q)
and
\beta\in(0,Q)
such that
\alpha+\beta<Q
. We recall that by (
27
), the convolution rule

P_{\alpha+\beta}=P_{\alpha}\star P_{\beta}

holds. For every
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
, exploiting the relation (
80
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\alpha+\beta}^{x}(\phi)

\displaystyle=P_{\alpha+\beta}(\phi\circ l_{x})=\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\alpha+\beta}(y)\phi(xy)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\alpha}\star P_{\beta}(y)\phi(xy)dy=\int_{\mathbb{G}}\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\alpha}(z)P_{\beta}(z^{-1}y)dz\phi(xy)dy

(94)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\alpha}(z)\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\beta}(z^{-1}y)\phi(xy)dydz=\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\alpha}(z)\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\beta}(z^{\prime})\phi(xzz^{\prime})dz^{\prime}dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}}P_{\alpha}(z)\psi_{\phi}(xz,\beta)dz=\widetilde{P}_{\alpha}^{x}(\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta))=\widetilde{P}_{\alpha}^{x}(\widetilde{P}_{\beta}^{xz}(\phi))=\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha).

We wish to show that the equality in (
3.6
) holds for every
\alpha,\beta\in(-2,Q)
such that
-2<\alpha+\beta<Q
. This conclude the proof by the uniqueness of analytic continuation.

First, we observe that, as a consequence of our analytic continuation, the map
\widetilde{P}_{\alpha+\beta}^{x}(\phi)=\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha+\beta)
is analytic with respect to
\alpha
and
\beta
on the set of
\alpha
and
\beta
such that
-2<\alpha+\beta<Q
.

Let us show that the map
\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)
is analytic as well with respect to
\alpha
and
\beta
for
\alpha,\beta\in(-2,Q)
,
\alpha+\beta<Q
.

In order to do this, we fix
\beta\in(-2,Q)
and consider
\alpha\to\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)
.
We need to apply the results of Proposition
3.2
and Section
3.2
. In fact we proved that, if one considers a map
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
such that
|\phi(z)|\leq K\|z\|_{c}^{-L}
when
\|z\|_{c}\geq S
for some
L,S,K>0
, then one can carry out the analytic continuation of the map
\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
for any
\alpha<\min\{L,Q\}
.
We apply this result to the map
\alpha\to\psi_{\psi(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)
. In fact, by the estimates of Section
3.5
we know that for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
we have
\psi_{\phi}(z,\beta)=O(\|z\|_{c}^{\beta-Q})
as
\|z\|_{c}\to\infty
.

Hence, by Proposition
3.2
, we conclude that
\alpha\to\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)
is analytic up to
-2<\alpha<\min\{Q-\beta,Q\}
, i.e. for every
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
such that
\alpha+\beta<Q
.

Clearly, the role of
\alpha
and
\beta
can be exchanged since
\widetilde{P}_{\alpha+\beta}^{x}(\phi)=\widetilde{P}_{\beta+\alpha}^{x}(\phi)
, and then
\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)=\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\alpha)}(x,\beta)
. Thus, for every
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
, the map
\beta\to\psi_{\psi(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)
is analytic for
\beta\in(-2,Q)
as
\alpha+\beta<Q
. To sum up, we proved that for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
,

(95)

\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha+\beta)=\widetilde{P}_{\alpha+\beta}^{x}(\phi)=\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)

hold for every
\alpha,\beta\in(-2,Q)
such that
-2<\alpha+\beta<Q
.

Let us now consider
x\in\mathbb{G}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
and set
\alpha,\beta\in(-2,Q)
such that
-2<\alpha+\beta<Q
. Set
\alpha=-2s
and
\beta=-2m
for suitable
s,m\in(-\frac{Q}{2},1)
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{s+m}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{P}^{x}_{\alpha+\beta}(\phi)=\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)=\psi_{\mathcal{L}^{m}\phi(\cdot)}(x,\alpha)=\mathcal{L}^{s}(\mathcal{L}^{m}\phi)(x).

This concludes the proof.
∎

4.
The Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{n}

In this section we recall the setting of the Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{n}
and we analyse some suitable families of integrals. This analysis will be an auxiliary tool to construct an analytic continuation of the map
\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-\infty,Q)
in the Heisenberg group.

4.1.
The Heisenberg group: definition and some properties

We discuss the Heisenberg group according to Section
2.1
, referring to the representation of a Carnot group
\mathbb{G}
in (
6
). For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, independently of the choice of
n
, the Heisenberg group can be represented as a vector space

\mathbb{H}^{n}=H_{1}\oplus H_{2},

with dim(
H_{1}
)
=2n
and dim(
H_{2}
)
=1
, endowed with a symplectic form
\omega
on
H_{1}
and a fixed nonvanishing element

(96)

e_{2n+1}\in H_{2}.

We denote by
\pi_{H_{1}}
and
\pi_{H_{2}}
the canonical projections on
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
, respectively, associated with the direct sum.
The space
\mathbb{H}^{n}
has a structure of Lie algebra given by

(97)

[x,y]=\omega(\pi_{H_{1}}(x),\pi_{H_{1}}(y))\ e_{2n+1}.

Then, by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, the Lie group product is

(98)

xy=x+y+\frac{[x,y]}{2}

for every
x,y\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
Notice that
q=2n+1
and
Q=2n+2
.
We fix a symplectic basis
(e_{1},\dots,e_{2n})
of
(H_{1},\omega)
, namely

\omega(e_{i},e_{n+j})=\delta_{ij},\qquad\omega(e_{i},e_{j})=\omega(e_{n+i},e_{n+j})=0,

for every
i,j=1,\ldots,n
, where
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta.
Thus, considering the vector
e_{2n+1}
given in (
96
), we obtain a basis

(99)

\mathcal{B}=(e_{1},\dots,e_{2n+1}).

We assume that on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
is fixed the scalar product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
that makes
\mathcal{B}
orthonormal.
We can read the product (
98
) on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
in coordinates with respect to
\mathcal{B}
(i.e. on
\mathbb{R}^{2n+1}
) as follows:

(100)

(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n+1})(y_{1},\dots,y_{2n+1})=\left(x_{1}+y_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1}+y_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{x_{i}y_{i+n}-x_{i+n}y_{i}}{2}\right),

for every
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1}),(y_{1},\ldots,y_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
Notice that
H_{2}=\mathrm{span}(e_{2n+1})
is the center of
\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
Moreover, taking in consideration the product (
100
), in our coordinates the Jacobian basis of left invariant vector fields is the following

(101)

\displaystyle X_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{j}}-\frac{1}{2}x_{j+n}\partial_{x_{2n+1}}\ \ \ \ \ \ j=1,\dots,n

\displaystyle Y_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{n+j}}+\frac{1}{2}x_{j}\partial_{x_{2n+1}}\ \ \ \ \ \ j=1,\dots,n

\displaystyle T(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{2n+1}},

for every
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
We will also use the following notation: for
i=1,\ldots,2n+1
we denote

\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{H}^{n})\ni Z_{i}:=\begin{cases}X_{i}\qquad\mathrm{if\ }i=1\ldots n\\
Y_{i-n}\qquad\mathrm{if\ }i=n+1,\ldots,2n\\
T\qquad\mathrm{if\ }i=2n+1.\\
\end{cases}

With a slight abuse of notation, we denote by
\pi_{H_{1}}
and
\pi_{H_{2}}
also the corresponding projections read in coordinates with respect to the basis
\mathcal{B}
i.e.

\displaystyle\pi_{H_{1}}:\mathbb{H}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n},\pi_{H_{1}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1})=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n}),

\displaystyle\pi_{H_{2}}:\mathbb{H}^{n}\to\mathbb{R},\pi_{H_{2}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1})=x_{2n+1},

for every
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.

The structure of the Lie algebra of
\mathbb{H}^{n}
reflects on the explicit form of the
Z
-Taylor polynomials. For example, we consider below the explicit form of the
Z
-Taylor Polynomial of
\mathbb{G}
-degree
n=3
related to a map
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
in the Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{n}
. Let
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, then for every
y\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, according to Theorem
2.10
, we have

(102)

\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)=&\phi(x)+\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Z_{i}\phi(x)y_{i}+T\phi(x)y_{2n+1}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)y_{i}y_{j}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Z_{i}T\phi(x)y_{i}y_{2n+1}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{2n}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{k}\phi(x)y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}.\end{split}

Moreover, by applying Theorem
2.9
, we get the existence of constants
c=c(\mathbb{H}^{n})>0
and
b=b(\mathbb{H}^{n})>0
such that

(103)

|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)|\leq c\|y\|_{c}^{4}\sup_{\{\|z\|_{c}\leq b^{4}\|y\|_{c}\}}\sup_{\{|\beta|=4\}}|(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{2n})^{\beta}\phi(xz)|,

for every
x,y\in\mathbb{G}
. Here
\beta
denotes a
2n
-multi-index.

We recall also some metric properties on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
. Referring to Theorem
2.7
, if
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{H}^{n}
, then the Carnot-Carathéodory distance
d_{c}(x,0)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2})
(see for example
[
1
, Lemma 3.11]
) and
|\nabla_{H}d_{c}(x,0)|=1
for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2}
.

In the following two subsections, we introduce some properties of
h
and
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
in
\mathbb{H}^{n}
.

4.2.
Invariance of the heat kernel under horizontal rotations

In many papers, like
[
22
,
26
,
23
,
32
]
,
the authors studied the heat kernel
h
in the Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{n}
and, in general, in stratified groups of step 2.
In particular, in the Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, for every
t>0
and
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, the heat kernel
h
depends on
t
, on the last component
x_{2n+1}
of
x
and on the norm
|(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n})|=|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|
.
This allows to deduce that the map
h
is invariant under the action of horizontal rotations, i.e. the rotations which do not involve the
x_{2n+1}
-axis
H_{2}
.

As a consequence, for every
t>0
, for every
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and for every
j\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
the equality

(104)

h(t,(x_{1},\dots,x_{j},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1}))=h(t,(x_{1},\dots,-x_{j},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1}))

holds.
Clearly, (
104
) yields that for every
\alpha<Q
, for every
t>0
and for every
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
the following equalities

\displaystyle\|(x_{1},\dots,x_{j},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1})\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}

\displaystyle=\|(x_{1},\dots,-x_{j},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1})\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q},

(105)

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(x_{1},\dots,x_{j},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1})

\displaystyle=P_{\alpha}(x_{1},\dots,-x_{j},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1})

hold for every
j\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
.
We notice that, a priori, this properties are not proved in arbitrary stratified groups, included the stratified groups of step 2. For the explicit form of the heat kernel we address the reader to
[
8
]
. For further details about this topic, we point out
[
7
, Remark 2.12]
.

4.3.
Invariance of
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
under horizontal rotations

In the Heisenberg group the Carnot-Carathéodory ball
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)
, of radius
R
, for
R>0
, centered at
0\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
is invariant under horizontal rotations, i.e. the rotations preserving the center of the group
H_{2}
(see for instance
[
28
, Section 2.3]
).

As a consequence, let
(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)
. Then, for every point
(y_{1},\dots,y_{2n},x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
such that the following equality

|(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n})|=|(y_{1},\dots,y_{2n})|

holds, then
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{2n},x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)
.
Let us now introduce for every
j\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
the following projection

(106)

\pi_{j}:H_{1}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n-1},\pi_{j}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2n}x_{i}e_{i}\right)=(x_{1},\dots,x_{j-1},x_{j+1},\dots,x_{2n},x_{2n}).

Notice that
\pi_{H_{2}}
and
\pi_{j}
, for
j=\{1,\dots,2n\}
, are Lipschitz map in the Euclidean sense and their (Euclidean) Jacobian is unitary. Hence, for every
r\in\pi_{H_{2}}(\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\subset\mathbb{R}
and for every
s=(s_{1},\dots,s_{2n-1})\in\pi_{j}(\pi_{H_{2}}^{-1}(r)\cap\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\subset\mathbb{R}^{2n-1}
, the invariance of
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)
with respect to horizontal rotations implies that
the set

(107)

\pi_{j}^{-1}(s)\cap\pi_{H_{2}}^{-1}(r)\cap\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)

is symmetric in the
x_{j}
-direction with respect to the point
(s_{1},\dots,s_{j-1},0,s_{j},\dots,s_{2n-1},r)
.

More precisely, for every
r\in\mathbb{R}
, every
s=(s_{1},\dots,s_{2n-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{2n-1}
and for every
x_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
:

(s_{1},\dots,s_{j-1},x_{j},s_{j},\dots,s_{2n-1},r)\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)\mathrm{\ if\ and\ only\ if\ }(s_{1},\dots,s_{j-1},-x_{j},s_{j},\dots,s_{2n-1},r)\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R).

In fact, the previous equivalence follows by

|(s_{1},\dots,s_{j-1},x_{j},s_{j},\dots,s_{2n-1})|=|(s_{1},\dots,s_{j-1},-x_{j},s_{j},\dots,s_{2n-1})|,

combined with the fact that
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,R)
is invariant under horizontal rotations.

In addition, also
\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
is invariant under horizontal rotations.

4.4.
Auxiliary tools

In this subsection we study some specific families of integrals. In particular, we collect some technical results which will be used later on in the paper.
First, we fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha<Q
and we collect few useful representations of the following integral

(108)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\big)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy.

We recall that the object
\mathcal{T}_{n}(\phi,x)
denotes the
Z
-Taylor Polynomial of
\mathbb{G}
-degree
n
related to
\phi
centered at
x
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

4.4.1.
Representation, I

First of all, we exploit the explicit representations of
\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)
and
\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)
given by Theorem
2.10
which allows to write the integral in (
108
) as

(109)

\sum_{h=2m}^{2m+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,h\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=h\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy.

Now, for
h\in\{2m,2m+1\}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,h\}
and
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1
such that
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=h
, we focus on the integral

(110)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

appearing in the sum (
109
).
We observe one more time that
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
and the map
\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}
are symmetric and invariant with respect to the group inversion. In fact,

•

by the homogeneity of
d_{c}
, if a point
y\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
, then
y^{-1}\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
.

•

by the property of the heat kernel
h
stated in Theorem
2.12
(iii), it holds that

\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}=\|y^{-1}\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q},

for every
y\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
,
y\neq 0
.

Moreover, for every
y\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
,
y^{-1}=-y
. Therefore, we know that, if the number
k\in\{1,\ldots,h\}
of the involved coordinates
y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}
is odd, then the integral in (
110
) vanishes

(111)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=0.

As a consequence, we can rewrite (
108
) as follows

(112)

\sum_{h=2m}^{2m+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,h,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=h\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy.

Now, we consider again the integral in (
110
)
and we make the following hypothesis

(113)

\mathrm{there\ exists\ }j\in\{1,\dots,k\}\mathrm{\ such\ that\ }\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{j}})=1\mathrm{\ and\ }\sharp\{\ell\in\{1,\dots,k\}:i_{j}=i_{\ell}\}\mathrm{\ is\ odd}.

Now, if we apply the Euclidean coarea formula to the integral in (
110
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=\int_{\pi_{H_{2}}(\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))}\int_{\pi_{H_{2}}^{-1}(y_{2n+1})\cap\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy_{1},\dots dy_{2n}\ dy_{2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\pi_{H_{2}}(\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))}\int_{\pi_{j}(\pi_{H_{2}}^{-1}(y_{2n+1})\cap\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))}\int_{\pi_{j}^{-1}(r)\cap\pi_{H_{2}}^{-1}(y_{2n+1})\cap\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy_{j}dr\ dy_{2n+1}.

By combining the symmetry of
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
discussed in Section
4.3
, the symmetry of
\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}
discussed in Section
4.2
and our hypothesis (
113
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\pi_{j}^{-1}(r)\cap\pi_{H_{2}}^{-1}(y_{2n+1})\cap\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy_{j}=0,

for every
y_{2n+1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
r\in\mathbb{R}^{2n-1}
.
Hence, under the hypothesis (
113
), the integral in (
110
) vanishes. Then, the integral in (
110
) can be non-zero only if

(114)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(y_{1},\dots,y_{2n})^{\gamma}y_{2n+1}^{\gamma_{2n+1}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy,

for some even
2n
-multi-index
\gamma\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{2n}
and for some
\gamma_{2n+1}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
.

Let us now consider again the integral in (
110
) and assume that
h=2m+1
. Hence we have that

(115)

\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m+1.

Since we are working in
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, for every
j=1,\dots,k
, necessarily
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{j}})
is 1 or 2. Hence

(116)

\sharp\{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}:\ \mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{j}})=1\}=2m+1-2\cdot\sharp\{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}:\ \mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{j}})=2\}.

Thus,
\sharp\{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}:\ \mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{j}})=1\}
is odd, and therefore hypothesis (
113
) is verified, hence, also under the hypothesis (
115
), we have

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=0.

Therefore, we can conclude that the integral in (
110
) can be non-zero only if

\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m.

As a consequence, in (
114
)
\gamma_{2n+1}
has to be even too. Hence, the integral in (
110
) can be non-zero only if

(117)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy,

for some even
(2n+1)
-multi-index
\gamma\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{2n+1}
.

We observe that our argument allows to state the equality

(118)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\big)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy.\end{split}

4.4.2.
Representation, II

According to the previous subsection, we fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,2m\}
and
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1
such that

\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m.

Applying the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr.

By the homogeneity of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}
and the homogeneity of
\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}
, we get for every
-2m<\alpha<Q

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\delta_{r}z)_{i_{1}}\ldots(\delta_{r}z)_{i_{k}}\|\delta_{r}z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}r^{Q-1}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}r^{\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})}z_{i_{1}}\ldots r^{\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})}z_{i_{k}}r^{\alpha-Q}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}r^{Q-1}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-Q+Q-1+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-1+2m}dr\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z).

Finally, for every
-2m<\alpha<Q
we can rewrite (
118
) as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\big)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

(119)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z).

As a consequence, for every
-2m<\alpha<Q
we also have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z).

4.4.3.
Regularity

Here, we prove that the following map is analytic

(120)

\begin{split}(-\infty,Q)\ni\alpha\to&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z).\end{split}

Let us first consider the map
\alpha\to\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}
. By the standard properties of the Euler gamma function we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-2m}\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-2m}\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha+2m}{2})}\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+(m-1)\right)\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+(m-2)\right)\ldots\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)=\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{m}m!,

then
\alpha\to\frac{1}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{R}
.
Indeed, it is enough to show that for every even
k\in\{1,\ldots,2m\}
and every
i_{1},\ldots i_{k}\leq 2n+1
such that
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m
, the map

(121)

\begin{split}(-\infty,Q)\ni\alpha\to\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)\end{split}

is analytic. The proof of this claim can be obtained as a generalization of the proof of Item
2
in Section
2.5
, where we showed the analyticity of the map
\sigma(\alpha)
, which in particular equals the map (
121
) when
m=0
. It is enough to repeat the cited proof combined with the fact that
z\to z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}
in (
120
) is a non-negative continuous map on the compact
\partial{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}
. In particular, the representation in (
117
) implies the non-negativity of this map.

4.4.4.
Representation, III

Here we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

For every even
(2n+1)
-multi-index
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{2n+1})\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{2n+1}
and for every
\alpha<Q
we have

(122)

\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}x^{\gamma}\|x\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(x)=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x^{\gamma}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}dx.

Proof.

We exploit the explicit form of
\|\cdot\|_{\alpha}
and the homogeneity property of the heat kernel
h
getting

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}h(t,y)dt\ d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dt.

Let us now perform the change of variable
r=\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}
so that
dt=-2r^{-3}dr
and we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dt

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\infty}^{0}(r^{-2})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1-\frac{Q}{2}}r^{-3}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{r}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr.

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{r}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-1-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}r^{\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1}}y^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{r}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-1-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\delta_{r}y)^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{r}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr.

Now we apply the change of variables
z=\delta_{r}(y)
and taking in account the homogeneity of the metric
d_{c}
and of the measure
\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}
, we get

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-1-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\delta_{r}(y))^{\gamma}h(1,\delta_{r}(y))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}z^{\gamma}h(1,z)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr.

Therefore, for every
\alpha<Q
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}z^{\gamma}h(1,z)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}z^{\gamma}h(1,z)\|z\|_{c}^{-\alpha-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}z^{\gamma}h(1,z)\|z\|_{c}^{-\alpha-(\gamma_{1}+\ldots+\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1})}dz,

where in the last step we exploited the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
. This concludes the proof.
∎

We recall that, if we fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,2m\}
and
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1
such that

\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m,

as in (
117
), then the existence of some even
(2n+1)
-multi-index
\gamma\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{2n+1}
such that the following equality holds

(123)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y^{\gamma}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

follows from Section
4.4.1
.
Thus, necessarily

\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m=\gamma_{1}+\ldots\gamma_{2n}+2\gamma_{2n+1}.

Therefore, exploiting Lemma
4.1
we can rewrite (
4.4.2
) for every
-2m<\alpha<Q
as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\big)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{\alpha+2m}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-2m}dy.

As a consequence, for every
-2m<\alpha<Q
we have that

(124)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-2m}dy.

4.4.5.
The behaviour as
\alpha\to-2\ell,\ \ell\leq m

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We wish to study the behaviour of the map

(125)

\displaystyle\alpha\to\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,\ k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-2m}dy,

when
\alpha
is close to an even negative integer
-2k
,
k\geq m
.
First, we compute the limit of (
125
) as
\alpha\to-2m^{+}
. We use the properties of the Euler gamma function getting

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,\ k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-2m}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,\ k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{\alpha+2m}\frac{\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+1\right)\ldots\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+(m-1)\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha+2m}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)dy

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,\ k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}(-1)^{m}m!\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)dy

as
\alpha\to-2m^{+}
.
Let us now consider
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
\ell<m
, then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-2\ell}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,\ k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{\alpha+2m}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-2m}dy\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-2\ell}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,\ k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{2m-2\ell}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{2\ell-2m}dy\right)=0.

4.4.6.
A second family of integrals

We study the regularity of the map

\alpha\to\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy.
The following result holds.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
x\in\mathbb{G}
. For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the map

(126)

\alpha\to\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

is analytic on the interval
(-2m-2,Q)
.

Before proving Lemma
4.2
, we focus on the convergence of (
126
).

Remark 4.3
.

We point out that
the integral

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

converges when
-2m-2<\alpha<Q
. In fact, exploiting the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
and the homogeneity properties of
P_{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}
we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(x\delta_{r}(z))-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(x\delta_{r}(z))\Big)P_{\alpha}(z)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)dr.

Now, recalling the regularity of
\phi
and the estimate (
13
) of Theorem
2.9
, we know that for every
z\in\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)

|(\phi(x\delta_{r}(z))-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(x\delta_{r}(z)))|=O(r^{2m+2})\quad\mathrm{as}\ r\to 0

holds. Therefore, for a suitable positive constant
c
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)||P_{\alpha}(y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{1}r^{\alpha+2m+1}dr\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|P_{\alpha}(z)|d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=c\ \frac{1}{|\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})|}\frac{1}{\alpha+2m+2}\sigma(\alpha),

which is finite. Here, we have exploited the fact that
\alpha>-2m-2
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

The proof is analogous to the one of Lemma
3.10
.
Let us consider the
k
-th derivative of the function in (
126
), for
k\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular, we will show that derivative and integral can be exchanged by estimating the term

(127)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|.

We split the integral of (
127
) as

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy))\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|\leq

(128)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy))\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy))\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|.

The second term can be dealt with repeating the steps in (
3.1
)
and we get that

(129)

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy))\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\ln^{k}(t)h(t,y)dtdy\right|\\
&\leq\sup_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(x\cdot)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(x\cdot)|\sup_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}h(1,\cdot)\mu(\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1))\frac{2}{(Q-\alpha)^{k+1}}k!.\end{split}

Then we need to deal with

\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy))\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|.

In fact, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}(\ln(t))^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy\right|

(130)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)|\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}h(t,y)dtdy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)|h(t,y)dydt.

Now, we observe that by the estimate (
13
) of Theorem
2.9
, we have
|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)|=O(\|y\|_{c}^{2m+2})
as
\|y\|_{c}\to 0
, thus for some suitable positive constant
C
we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)|h(t,y)dydt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(t,y)dydt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}t^{-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y))dydt,

where we exploited the homogeneity property of
h
. Now, we change variables as
z=\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y)
, so that by the homogeneity of the Lebesgue measure and the properties of homogeneous norms we con continue as follows

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}t^{-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\|y\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(y))dydt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}})}\|\delta_{\sqrt{t}}(z)\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(1,z)dzdt

(131)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}})}t^{m+1}\|z\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(1,z)dzdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+m}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}dt\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\|z\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(1,z)dz.

Notice that
\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\|z\|_{c}^{2m+2}h(1,z)dz<\infty
is a finite constant, since
h(1,z)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
. Now, we observe that, according to (
32
),

b_{k}=\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+m}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}dt

when
N=m+1
, and
N=m+1>-\frac{\alpha}{2}
if and only if
\alpha>-(2m+2)
. Hence, we can exploit the estimate in (
33
) getting that for every fixed
\alpha>-2m-2
, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}

(132)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+m}|\ln(t)|^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}dt\leq C\frac{2}{(\alpha+2m+2)^{k+1}}k!.\end{split}

By combining (
129
), (
4.4.6
), (
4.4.6
) and (
132
) our claim is achieved.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

The results of this subsection are still true if
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
\phi(z)\leq K\|z\|_{c}^{-L}
as
\|z_{c}\|>S
for some
L,S,K>0
. It can be verified that in this case the map in (
126
) is analytic on
(-2m-2,\min\{Q,L\})
.

5.
Analytic continuation in the Heisenberg group

Here, we prove the following result.

Theorem 5.1
.

For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{G})
and for every
x\in\mathbb{G}
, the map

\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)

can be analytically continued to the interval
(-2m-2,-2m]
. Moreover, the representation

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

(133)

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

holds for every
\alpha
such that
-2m-2<\alpha\leq Q
.

The proof of the previous theorem will be realized by induction in the following two subsections.

5.1.
Base case: continuation of
\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
on the strip
(-4,-2]

We consider the map
\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
which has been analytically continued to
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
in Theorem
3.8
. We obtained the expression of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-2,Q)

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\phi(x)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

(134)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

on an arbitrary stratified group
\mathbb{G}
, and then, in particular, it holds on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
. In this subsection, we wish to continue analytically the map on the strip
-4<\alpha\leq 2
, so as to show the base step of the inductive procedure. In corresponds to the value
m=1
.
We consider
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
and we consider (
5.1
) as follows

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{1}(\phi,x)(xy))P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{1}(\phi,x)(xy))P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{1}(\phi,x)(xy))P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha).

We can exploit the expression obtained in (
4.4.2
) and for every
\alpha\in(-2,Q)
we get

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

(135)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{2}})=2\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}Z_{i_{2}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha).

Let us focus on the right-hand side of (
5.1
). We notice that, by Remark
3.5
, the first term of the sum

\alpha\to\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
. The last term of the sum

\alpha\to\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
too, since in Item
2
of Section
2.5
we proved that
\sigma(\alpha)
is analytic on
(-\infty,\alpha)
. By the results in Section
4.4.3
, the third term of the sum

\alpha\to\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{2}})=2\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}Z_{i_{2}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
. Finally, by Lemma
4.2
applied with
m=1
, the second term of the sum

\alpha\to\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{3}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

is analytic on
(-4,Q)
. In conclusion, the map
\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
is analytic on
(-4,Q)
. Then, we have obtained an analytic continuation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
-4<\alpha\leq-2
. On the other hand, we have proved the first step of the inductive procedure, corresponding to
m=1
, aimed in the current section.

5.2.
Induction step: continuation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on the strip
(-2m-2,-2m]

Now, we fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and we assume that the map
\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
has been analytically continued on
(-2m,Q)
and that the representation

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2(m-1)+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

(136)

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

holds.
We wish to construct an analytic continuation of the map
\psi(x,\alpha)
in (
5.2
) on the strip
(-2m-2,-2m]
. Let us consider

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

(137)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2(m-1)+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha).

Now, we apply (
4.4.2
) and we get that for every
\alpha\in(-2m,Q)
, the right-hand side of (
5.2
) is equal to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

(138)

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha).

Let us focus on (
5.2
). We observe that the first term of the sum is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
by Remark
3.5
; the last term of the sum is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
since in Item
2
of Section
2.5
we proved that
\sigma(\alpha)
is analytic on
(-\infty,\alpha)
; the third term of the sum is analytic on
(-\infty,Q)
by the results in Section
4.4.3
; the second term of the sum is analytic on
(-2m-2,Q)
by Lemma
4.2
. Then, the map
\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
in (
5.2
) is analytic on
(-2m-2,Q)
. Hence, we provided an analytic continuation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
-2m-2<\alpha\leq-2m
and the representation in (
5.2
) holds for
-2m-2<\alpha<Q
.
Hence the induction is concluded and Theorem
5.1
is proved.

Remark 5.2
.

Notice that, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, when
\alpha<-2m
, by explicit calculations based on the coarea formula, we obtain

(139)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m-1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy,\end{split}

so that, when
-2m-2<\alpha<-2m
we have the following representation of the analytic continuation

(140)

\begin{split}\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy.\end{split}

5.2.1.
Analysis of
\psi(x,-2m)
,
m\in\mathbb{N}

Let us now compute the value
\psi(x,-2m)
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We exploit the representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-2m-2,Q)
and the results in Section
4.4.5
getting:

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy+\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-2m}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2p,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2p}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{k}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}(-1)^{m}m!\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)dy

as
\alpha
goes to
-2m
. We used in the last two steps the results of Section
4.4.5
. Hence, we showed that for every
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\psi(x,-2m)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1,\ldots,2m,k\ \mathrm{even}\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\ldots\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{k}})=2m\end{subarray}}(-1)^{m}m!\frac{Z_{i_{1}}\ldots Z_{i_{k}}\phi(x)}{k!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{i_{1}}\ldots y_{i_{k}}h(1,y)dy.

5.3.
An estimate of
x\to\psi(x,\alpha)

We wish to use the analytic continuation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
to extend the notion of fractional sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}^{s}
in the spirit of Sections
3.4
and
3.6
. In order to do this, we need to generalize in
\mathbb{H}^{n}
the estimate on
x\to\psi(x,\alpha)
established in Section
3.5
. This is the goal of the following proposition.

Proposition 5.3
.

For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, for every
\alpha\in(-2m-2,2m)
, for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
the following estimate holds

\psi(x,\alpha)=O(\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-Q})

as
\|x\|_{c}\to\infty
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the one of Proposition
3.16
. We exploit the expression of
\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{2m+1}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

obtained in (
5.1
) for
-2m-2<\alpha<-2m
. Thus, the proof of Proposition
3.16
can be essentially repeated verbatim, up to substituting
b^{2}
with
b^{2m+2}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{h}^{2}(\xi)
with

(141)

\mathfrak{d}^{2m+2}_{h}\phi(\xi)=\max_{p\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,b^{m+2}\|x^{-1}z\|_{c})}\mathfrak{d}^{2m+2}_{h}\phi(p),

where
\mathfrak{d}^{2m+2}_{h}\phi
is
\mathfrak{d}^{2m+2}_{h}\phi(y)=\max_{|\alpha|=2m+2}|(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{2n})^{\alpha}\phi(y)|
, for every
y\in\mathbb{G}
.

More precisely, in this case the integral variable
z
belongs to the closed ball
\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2b^{2m+2}})
and for each of such a
z
, the maximum of
\mathfrak{d}_{h}^{2m+2}
in (
141
) is realized at
\xi=\xi(z)\in\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,b^{2m+2}\|x^{-1}z\|)\subset\mathbb{B}_{c}(x,\frac{\|x\|_{c}}{2})
.
Moreover, it is necessary to exploit the fact that every derivative along vector fields of
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, independently of the number of involved vector fields and on their degree, belongs to
S(\mathbb{H}^{n}
), hence
\mathfrak{d}_{h}^{2m+2}\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
.
∎

5.4.
The powers of the fractional sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}^{s}
,
-\frac{Q}{2}<s<m+1
,
m\in\mathbb{N}

Coherently with the construction of the analytic continuation of the map
\alpha\to\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
on the interval
(-2m-2,Q)
, for an arbitrary
m\in\mathbb{N}
, in this section we extend the definition of real powers of the sub-Laplacian
