\mathcal{L}
to
s\in(-\frac{Q}{2},m+1)
. In fact, for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
the analytic continuation of
\alpha\to\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
on the interval
\alpha\in(-2m-2,Q)
allows to extend the definition of the family of tempered distributions
\{\widetilde{P}^{x}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in(-2,Q)}
that we introduced in Section
3.4
, setting for every
\alpha\in(-2m-2,Q)
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
the following

\widetilde{P}^{x}_{\alpha}(\phi):=\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
.

Therefore, we can define for every
s\in(-\frac{Q}{2},m+1)
the fractional sub-Laplacian

(142)

\mathcal{L}^{s}\phi(x):=\psi_{\phi}(x,-2s)=\widetilde{P}^{x}_{-2s}(\phi),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.

In a similar way as in Section
3.6
, the uniqueness of analytic continuation yields that for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
the equality

(143)

\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha+\beta)=\widetilde{P}_{\alpha+\beta}^{x}(\phi)=\psi_{\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\beta)}(x,\alpha)

holds for every
\alpha\in(-2m-2,Q),\beta\in(-2m-2,Q)
such that
-2m-2<\alpha+\beta<Q
.
Therefore, one more time through analogous computations as in Section
3.6
, (
143
) allows to prove that if we choose
\alpha,\beta\in(-2m-2,Q)
and we set
\alpha=-2s
and
\beta=-2p
for suitable
s,p\in(-\frac{Q}{2},m+1)
, then the equality

(144)

\mathcal{L}^{s}\circ\mathcal{L}^{p}=\mathcal{L}^{s+p}

holds, when
-\frac{Q}{2}<s+p<m+1
.

Since for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, the analytic continuation of
\alpha\to\psi_{\phi}(x,\alpha)
has been realized on the interval
(-2m-2,Q)
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, then (
144
) holds for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, we can deduce that the operator
\mathcal{L}^{s}
is defined as in (
142
) for every
s>-\frac{Q}{2}
and it satisfies the semigroup property (
145
) for every
s,p>-\frac{Q}{2}
such that
s+q>-\frac{Q}{2}
. We resume this result in the following proposition.

Proposition 5.4
.

For every
s,p\in(-\frac{Q}{2},\infty)
such that
-\frac{Q}{2}<p+s
the equality

(145)

\mathcal{L}^{s}\circ\mathcal{L}^{p}=\mathcal{L}^{s+p}

holds.

Actually, recalling the homogeneous dimension of
\mathbb{H}^{n}
is
Q=2n+2
, we have introduced a notion of
\mathcal{L}^{s}
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, for
s>-n-1
, which satisfies the semigroup property (
145
) for every
s,p>-n-1
such that
s+p>-n-1
.

6.
A representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-4,Q)

In this section we obtain a special simplified representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on the interval
(-4,Q)
. Then, we use this representation to deduce the value of the integral
\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,x)dx
.

Let us observe that, according to the arguments in Section
4.4.1
leading to (
114
) and then to (
117
), for every
\alpha<Q
the equality

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{2}})=2\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}Z_{i_{2}}\phi(x)}{2!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

(146)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Z^{2}_{i}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

holds.
Now, since the ball
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
and
\|\cdot\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}
are invariant under horizontal rotations (see Sections
4.3
and
4.2
), then the relation

(147)

d(\alpha):=\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)=\frac{1}{2n}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(y)|^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

holds for every
i=1,\dots,2n
. In fact, by exploiting horizontal rotation invariance for every
i=1,\ldots,2n
, we get that

(148)

\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)=\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{j}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y),

for every
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,2n\}
. Then, we have that for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,2n\}

\displaystyle 2n\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2n}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}y_{i}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|(y_{1},\ldots,y_{2n})|^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(y)|^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y).

As a consequence, the value of
d(\alpha)
does not depend on the choice of
i
.
We can continue from (
6
) getting

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\leq 2n+1\\
\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{1}})+\mathrm{deg}(Z_{i_{2}})=2\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{Z_{i_{1}}Z_{i_{2}}\phi(x)}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}\|z\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Z^{2}_{i}\phi(x)d(\alpha)=-\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha).

Thus, the analytically continued map
(-4,Q)\ni\alpha\to\psi(x,\alpha)
can be represented as follows

(149)

\begin{split}\psi(x,\alpha)=&\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Z^{2}_{i}\phi(x)y^{2}_{i}\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy\\
&+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha).\end{split}

The value of
\psi(x,-2)
can be deduced by combining Proposition
2.13
and the equality in (
79
) getting that

(150)

\lim_{\alpha\to-2}\psi(x,\alpha)=\lim_{\alpha\to-2^{+}}\psi(x,\alpha)=\lim_{\alpha\to-2^{+}}\mathcal{L}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\phi(x)=\mathcal{L}\phi(x).

6.1.
A geometric application, I

We use the representation established in (
149
) to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let
h
be the heat kernel associated with
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, then the following equality holds

(151)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,x)dx=4n.

Proof.

Let us consider the representation (
149
) of the map
\psi(x,\alpha)
for
-4<\alpha<Q

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\phi(x)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Z^{2}_{i}\phi(x)y^{2}_{i}\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha).

By the arguments carried out in Section
6
we know that

\displaystyle\psi(x,-2)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\alpha\to-2}\psi(x,\alpha)=\lim_{\alpha\to-2}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha+2}{2})}\frac{\alpha}{2}d(\alpha)\mathcal{L}\phi(x)\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\alpha\to-2}\frac{1}{4}d(\alpha)\mathcal{L}\phi(x)=\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)\lim_{\alpha\to-2}d(\alpha),

and we know as well that the limit exists since
d(\alpha)
is continuous with respect to
\alpha
as
\alpha<Q
.
Now, as we already observed, by Proposition
2.13
and (
79
), we get

(152)

\psi(x,-2)=\lim_{\alpha\to-2^{+}}\psi(x,\alpha)=\lim_{\alpha\to-2^{+}}\mathcal{L}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\phi(x)=\mathcal{L}\phi(x),

so that we can deduce that

(153)

\lim_{\alpha\to-2}d(\alpha)=d(-2)=4.

Let us now consider for
\alpha<Q
, the map
d(\alpha)
defined in (
147
). We have

\displaystyle d(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}\|x\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}h(t,x)dt\ d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(t,x)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dt.

Performing the change of variable
r=\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1-\frac{Q}{2}}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dt

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2n}2\int_{\infty}^{0}(r^{-2})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1-\frac{Q}{2}}r^{-3}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,\delta_{r}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,\delta_{r}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-3}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|r\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,\delta_{r}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dr.

Let us remark that for every
r>0
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
we have
|r\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}=|\pi_{H_{1}}(\delta_{r}(x))|^{2}
, thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-3}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|r\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,\delta_{r}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-3}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(\delta_{r}(x))|^{2}h(1,\delta_{r}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dr.

By changing one more time the variables
x^{\prime}=\delta_{r}(x)
, the homogeneity of the metric
d_{c}
and of the measure
\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}
give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha+Q-3}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(\delta_{r}(x))|^{2}h(1,\delta_{r}(x))d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x)dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha-2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x^{\prime})|^{2}h(1,x^{\prime})d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x^{\prime})dr.

Our thesis follows as
\alpha\to-2
. In fact, by (
153
) we know that

(154)

\lim_{\alpha\to-2}d(\alpha)=d(-2)=4

but, at the same time, we know as well that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-2}d(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\alpha\to-2}\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha-2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x^{\prime})|^{2}h(1,x^{\prime})d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x^{\prime})dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,r)}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x^{\prime})|^{2}h(1,x^{\prime})d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(x^{\prime})dr

(155)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x^{\prime})|^{2}h(1,x^{\prime})d(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{2}h(1,x)dx,

where we used the coarea formula of Corollary
2.8
.
By combining (
154
) and (
6.1
) we conclude the proof.
∎

7.
A representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-6,Q)

Here we discuss a representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
for
-6<\alpha\leq-4
involving the most natural definition of the natural power
\mathcal{L}^{2}
of the sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}
.
Given
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we define

(156)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{m}\phi:

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\phi\quad\mathrm{if\ }m=1,

(157)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{m}\phi:

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{L}^{m-1}\phi)\quad\mathrm{if\ }m>1,

for every
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
.
An explicit computation on the Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{n}
shows that

\mathcal{L}^{m}\phi(x)=(-1)^{m}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,2n\}}Z_{i_{1}}^{2}\ldots Z_{i_{m}}^{2}\phi(x),

for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
.

According to the continuation described in Sections
5
and
6
and to the explicit form of the
Z
-Taylor polynomials given by Theorem
2.10
, we recall that
\psi(x,\alpha)
can be represented for
\alpha\in(-6,Q)
as follows:

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4!}\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\leq 2n\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\Big(Z^{2}_{i}Z^{2}_{j}\phi(x)+Z^{2}_{j}Z^{2}_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)

(158)

\displaystyle+Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)\Big)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}y_{j}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4!}\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\sum_{i\leq 2n}Z^{4}_{i}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{4}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+4}T^{2}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y).

For every
i,j\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
, with
i<j
, there are only two possibilities

(a)

[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=0
, hence
Z_{i}Z_{j}=Z_{j}Z_{i}
. In this case we can state the following equality

\displaystyle Z^{2}_{i}Z^{2}_{j}\phi(x)+Z^{2}_{j}Z^{2}_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)

(159)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+3Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x).

(b)

[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=T
, hence
Z_{i}Z_{j}=Z_{j}Z_{i}+T
and
Z_{j}Z_{i}=Z_{i}Z_{j}-T
.

We recall also that
[Z_{i},T]=0
for every
i\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
.

Let us assume that we are in case (b). Thus, we have

\displaystyle Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)=Z_{i}(Z_{i}Z_{j}-T)Z_{j}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)-Z_{i}Z_{j}T\phi(x)

\displaystyle Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)=Z_{j}(Z_{j}Z_{i}+T)Z_{i}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}T\phi(x)

\displaystyle Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)=(Z_{j}Z_{i}+T)Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}T\phi(x)=Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)+2Z_{j}Z_{i}T\phi(x)

\displaystyle Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)=(Z_{i}Z_{j}-T)Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)-Z_{i}Z_{j}T\phi(x)=Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)-2Z_{i}Z_{j}T\phi(x).

Therefore,

\displaystyle Z^{2}_{i}Z^{2}_{j}\phi(x)+Z^{2}_{i}Z^{2}_{j}\phi(x)+Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{j}Z_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{j}Z_{i}Z_{i}Z_{j}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+3Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)+3Z_{j}Z_{i}T\phi(x)-3Z_{i}Z_{j}T\phi(x)

(160)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+3Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)-3(Z_{i}Z_{j}-Z_{j}Z_{i})T\phi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+3Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)-3T^{2}\phi(x).

We can then use (
(a)
) and (
7
) to rewrite (
7
) as

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

(161)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4!}\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\leq 2n\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\big(Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)\big)3\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4!}\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\sum_{i\leq 2n}Z^{4}_{i}\phi(x)\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+T^{2}\phi(x)\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\left(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)-\frac{3n}{4!}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\right).

In order to write (
7
), we used the fact that there are
n
couples of indexes
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,2n\}
satisfying case (b). Moreover, we have exploited the invariance by horizontal rotation of
\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)
and of the
P_{\alpha}
, which ensure that the equalities

(162)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{4}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)=\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{j}^{4}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\qquad\mathrm{for\ every\ }i,j\in\{1,

\displaystyle\dots,2n\},

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{2}y_{j}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)=\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{\ell}^{2}y_{m}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\qquad\mathrm{for\ every\ }i\neq j,\ell\neq m\in

\displaystyle\{1,\dots,2n\}.

hold.

Now, by applying Lemma
4.1
for an arbitrary
\alpha<Q
, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}z_{1}^{4}h(1,z)\|z\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dz,

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}|\pi_{H_{2}}(z)|^{2}h(1,z)\|z\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dz;

therefore, for
\alpha=-4
we obtain

(163)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{-4-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}z_{1}^{4}h(1,z)dz

(164)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{-4-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}|\pi_{H_{2}}(z)|^{2}h(1,z)dz.

As a consequence, we deduce that for every
-6<\alpha<Q
the following representation holds

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

(165)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\leq 2n\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\Big(Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)\Big)6\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{4!}\sum_{i\leq 2n}Z^{4}_{i}\phi(x)\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{4}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy

\displaystyle+T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{2n+1}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy-\frac{6n}{4!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy\bigg)\Bigg).

In order to obtain a simpler representation, we need the following result.

Proposition 7.1
.

For every
\alpha<Q
the following equality holds

(166)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{4}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy=3\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy.

Proof.

Following
[
2
, Section 4]
(see also
[
24
]
), we first introduce suitable polar coordinates in
\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2}
.

\displaystyle T:(0,\infty)\times\left(-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\times[0,\pi)^{2n-2}\times[0,2\pi)

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2}

\displaystyle(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})

\displaystyle\to T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}),

with

\displaystyle T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})=\begin{bmatrix}\rho\sqrt{\cos\varphi}\cos\theta_{1},\\
\rho\sqrt{\cos\varphi}\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2},\\
\rho\sqrt{\cos\varphi}\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos\theta_{3},\\
\ldots\\
\rho\sqrt{\cos\varphi}\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\ldots\sin\theta_{2n-2}\cos\theta_{2n-1},\\
\rho\sqrt{\cos\varphi}\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\ldots\sin\theta_{2n-2}\sin\theta_{2n-1}\\
\rho^{2}\sin\varphi\end{bmatrix}^{T}.

According to our purposes, we do not need to an explicit computation of the Jacobian of the transformation
T
. We just notice that, by the computations in
[
2
, Section 4]
, the Jacobian does not depend on
\theta_{2n-1}
and we denote it by

JT_{\overline{\theta_{2n-1}}}(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{2n-2},\theta_{2n-1}),

in order to emphasize its independence of
\theta_{2n-1}
.
By the invariance of
h(1,\cdot)
with respect to horizontal rotations (see (
162
)), to prove (
166
) means to prove that

(167)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{2n}^{4}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy=3\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{2n-1}^{2}y_{2n}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy.

Let us first perform the change of variables
T
on the integral appearing on the right-hand side of (
167
) as follows

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{2n-1}^{2}y_{2n}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2}}y_{2n-1}^{2}y_{2n}^{2}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\rho^{4}\cos^{2}\varphi\sin^{4}\theta_{1}\sin^{4}\theta_{2}\ldots\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-2}\cos^{2}\theta_{2n-1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2n-1}

\displaystyle h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}JT_{\overline{\theta_{2n-1}}}(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{2n-2},\theta_{2n-1})d\theta_{2n-1}\ldots d\theta_{1}d\varphi d\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{4}\int_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\cos^{2}\varphi\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin^{4}\theta_{1}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-2}JT_{\overline{\theta_{2n-1}}}(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{2n-2},\theta_{2n-1})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{2}\theta_{2n-1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}d\theta_{2n-1}d\theta_{2n-2}\ldots d\theta_{1}d\varphi d\rho.

Now we apply the change of variables
T
on the integral on the left-hand side of (
167
) and we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{2n}^{4}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2}}y_{2n}^{4}h(1,y)\|y\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\rho^{4}\cos^{2}\varphi\sin^{4}\theta_{1}\sin^{4}\theta_{2}\ldots\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-2}\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-1}

\displaystyle h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}JT_{\overline{\theta_{2n-1}}}(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{2n-2},\theta_{2n-1})d\theta_{2n-1}\ldots d\theta_{1}d\varphi d\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{4}\int_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\cos^{2}\varphi\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin^{4}\theta_{1}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-2}JT_{\overline{\theta_{2n-1}}}(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{2n-2},\theta_{2n-1})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}d\theta_{2n-1}d\theta_{2n-2}\ldots d\theta_{1}d\varphi d\rho.

Therefore, in order to prove (
167
), it is enough to prove that, for fixed
\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-2}\in(0,\infty)\times\left(-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\times(0,\pi)^{2n-2}
it holds that

(168)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}d\theta_{2n-1}\\
=&\ 3\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{2}\theta_{2n-1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}d\theta_{2n-1}.\end{split}

Notice that
\|\cdot\|_{c}
is a
C^{1}
map on
\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus H_{2}
(see for example
[
1
, Lemma 3.11]
) and that
h(1,z)
is
C^{\infty}
on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
. Now, exploiting the integration by parts, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{2}\theta_{2n-1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}d\theta_{2n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\sin^{3}\theta_{2n-1}}{3}\cos\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}\right]_{\theta_{2n-1}=0}^{\theta_{2n-1}=2\pi}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{\sin^{3}\theta_{2n-1}}{3}\Bigg(-\sin\theta_{2n-1}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\cos\theta_{2n-1}\frac{d}{d\theta_{2n-1}}\Big(h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}\Big)\Bigg)d\theta_{2n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{\sin^{4}\theta_{2n-1}}{3}h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}d\theta_{2n-1}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{\sin^{3}\theta_{2n-1}}{3}\cos\theta_{2n-1}\frac{d}{d\theta_{2n-1}}\Big(h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}\Big)d\theta_{2n-1}.

Thus, (
168
) follows if we prove that

\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{\sin^{3}\theta_{2n-1}}{3}\cos\theta_{2n-1}\frac{d}{d\theta_{2n-1}}\Big(h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}\Big)d\theta_{2n-1}=0.

We will show the previous equality by showing that

(169)

\frac{d}{d\theta_{2n-1}}\Big(h(1,T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}\Big)=0

for every
\rho,\varphi,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}\in(0,\infty)\times\left(-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\times(0,\pi)^{2n-2}\times(0,2\pi)
.
In particular, it is enough to notice that, once fixed
\bar{\rho},\bar{\varphi},\bar{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{2n-2}\in(0,\infty)\times\left(-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\times[0,\pi)^{2n-2}
, the map

[0,2\pi)\ni\theta_{2n+1}\to T(\bar{\rho},\bar{\varphi},\bar{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{2n-2},\theta_{2n-1})

is a horizontal rotation. On the other hand, we argue as follows

•

the heat kernel
h(t,y)
is invariant with respect to horizontal rotations, as stated in Section
4.2
, thus
h(1,(z_{1},\tau_{1}))=h(1,(z_{2},\tau_{2}))
if
|z_{1}|=|z_{2}|
, for every
(z_{1},\tau_{1}),(z_{2},\tau_{2})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, with
z_{1},z_{2}\in H_{1}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{2n}
and
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in H_{2}\simeq\mathbb{R}
.

•

the norm
\|\cdot\|_{c}
is invariant with respect to horizontal rotations, as stated in Section
4.3
, thus if we have
(z_{1},\tau),(z_{2},\tau)\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, with
|z_{1}|=|z_{2}|
, then
\|(z_{1},\tau)\|_{c}=\|(z_{2},\tau)\|_{c}
.

Hence for every
(\bar{\rho},\bar{\varphi},\bar{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{2n-2})\in(0,\infty)\times\left(-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\times[0,\pi)^{2n-2}
, the map

(0,2\pi)\ni\theta_{2n+1}\to h(1,T(\bar{\rho},\bar{\varphi},\bar{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}))\|T(\bar{\rho},\bar{\varphi},\bar{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-4}

is a constant map, then (
169
) holds. This concludes the proof.
∎

By combining Proposition
7.1
with Lemma
4.1
, the following corollary holds.

Corollary 7.2
.

For every
\alpha<Q
the following equality holds

(170)

\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)=3\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y).

By combining Corollary
7.2
and (
7
) we have that for
\alpha\in(-6,Q)

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\leq 2n\\
i<j\end{subarray}}(Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x))3\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{i\leq 2n}Z^{4}_{i}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)-\frac{3n}{4!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\leq 2n\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\Big(Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)+Z^{4}_{i}\phi(x)+Z_{j}^{4}\phi(x)\Big)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)-\frac{n}{4!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\bigg)\Bigg)

(171)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{i,j\leq 2n}\Big(Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)\Big)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)-\frac{n}{4!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\bigg)\Bigg).

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{5}(\phi,x)(xy))P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4!}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)-\frac{n}{4!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\bigg)\Bigg).

7.1.
A geometric application, II

We use the representation obtained in the previous subsection in (
7
) to deduce the value of some integrals.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let
h
be the heat kernel associated with
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, then for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,2n\}
the following equalities hold

(172)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{i}^{4}h(1,x)dx=12\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{2n+1}^{2}h(1,x)dx=n.

Proof.

According to the representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-6,Q)
obtained in (
7
), exploiting the definition of real power of the fractional sub-Laplacian in (
142
), we get that

(173)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(x)&=\lim_{\alpha\to-4}\psi(x,\alpha)\\
&=\frac{2}{4!}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(x)\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{4}h(1,y)dy+T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{2n+1}^{2}h(1,y)dy-\frac{2n}{4!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{4}h(1,y)dy\bigg).\end{split}

The equalities in (
172
) will follow from (
173
) if we show that it does not exist any constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that the equality

(174)

\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(x)=cT^{2}\phi(x)

holds for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
Let us assume by contradiction that (
174
) holds and let us denote by
d_{\lambda}
, for every
\lambda>0
, the isotropic dilation by
\lambda
, i.e.

d_{\lambda}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1})=(\lambda x_{1},\ldots,\lambda x_{2n+1}),

for every
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
Now, we observe that for every
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, for every
\lambda>0
the following homogeneity property holds

T^{2}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x))=\lambda^{2}T^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x)).

Taking in consideration that, if
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, for every
\lambda>0
the map
\phi\circ d_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, then we can apply (
174
) to get that

(175)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x)

\displaystyle=cT^{2}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x)=c\lambda^{2}T^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))=\lambda^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x)),

for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
On the other hand, by the homogeneity property of
\mathcal{L}^{2}
with respect to the group dilations
\delta_{\lambda}
we can continue from (
175
) getting

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{\lambda})^{4}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}(\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}}d_{\lambda}(x))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})(\delta_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}}(d_{\lambda}(x)))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})(\sqrt{\lambda}\pi_{H_{1}}(x),\pi_{H_{2}}(x)).

Thus, we established the relation

(176)

\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x)=\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})(\sqrt{\lambda}\pi_{H_{1}}(x),\pi_{H_{2}}(x)),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
,
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and
\lambda>0
.
Let us show that the homogeneity property obtained in (
176
) cannot be true. By straightforward computations it is possible to verify that for every
\lambda>0
,
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
we have the following representation

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x)=\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{L}(\phi\circ d_{\lambda}))(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{L}\Big(\lambda^{2}(\Delta\phi\circ d_{\lambda})+\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(x_{i}(\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ d_{\lambda})-y_{i}(\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ d_{\lambda})+\frac{1}{4}(x_{i}^{2}+y_{i}^{2})(\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\circ d_{\lambda})\big)\Big)(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{2}\mathcal{L}(\Delta\phi\circ d_{\lambda})(x)+\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}\big(x_{i}(\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ d_{\lambda})-y_{i}(\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ d_{\lambda})\big)(x)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}((x_{i}^{2}+y_{i}^{2})(\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\circ d_{\lambda}))(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{4}\Delta^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+n\lambda^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+P_{1}(\lambda,x,\phi),

where the second addend in the last equality comes from the computation of the term
\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}((x_{i}^{2}+y_{i}^{2})(\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\circ d_{\lambda}))(x)
and
P_{1}(\lambda,x,\phi)=o(\lambda^{2})
as
\lambda\to 0^{+}.
Here, we have denoted by
\Delta
the operator

\Delta=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}+\partial_{y_{i}}^{2}).

On the other hand, we have as well for every
z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})(z)=\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{L}(\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}))(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{L}\Big(\lambda(\Delta\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})+\lambda\sqrt{\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(x_{i}(\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})-y_{i}(\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}))+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big((x_{i}^{2}+y_{i}^{2})(\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})\big)\Big)(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda\mathcal{L}(\Delta\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})(z)+\lambda\sqrt{\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}\big(x_{i}(\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})-y_{i}(\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\circ d_{\lambda})\big)(z)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}((x_{i}^{2}+y_{i}^{2})(\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}))(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{2}\Delta^{2}\phi(\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}(z))+n\lambda^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\phi(\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}(z))+P_{2}(\lambda,z,\phi),

where the second addend in the last equality comes from the computation of the term
\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}((x_{i}^{2}+y_{i}^{2})(\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}}))(z)
and
P_{2}(\lambda,z,\phi)=o(\lambda^{2})
as
\lambda\to 0^{+}.

When we evaluate the previous operator at a point of the form
z=(\sqrt{\lambda}\pi_{H_{1}}(x),\pi_{H_{2}}(x))
, for an arbitrary
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, we obtain

\mathcal{L}^{2}(\phi\circ\delta_{\sqrt{\lambda}})(\sqrt{\lambda}\pi_{H_{1}}(x),\pi_{H_{2}}(x))=\lambda^{2}\Delta^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+n\lambda^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+P_{2}(\lambda,x,\phi).

Therefore, if (
176
) holds, for every
\lambda>0
,
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
we also have

(177)

\lambda^{4}\Delta^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+n\lambda^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\phi(\delta_{\lambda}(x))+P_{1}(\lambda,x,\phi)=\lambda^{2}\Delta^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+n\lambda^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\phi(d_{\lambda}(x))+P_{2}(\lambda,x,\phi).

Now, dividing both sides of (
177
) by
\lambda^{2}
and letting
\lambda
go to zero, we deduce that for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})

(178)

\Delta^{2}\phi(0)=0,

but (
178
) does not hold because if
\phi(x)=e^{-\|x\|_{K}^{4}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, where for
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
we denoted by

\|x\|_{K}=\sqrt[4]{|\pi_{H_{1}}(x)|^{4}+|\pi_{H_{2}}(x)|^{2}}

the norm arising from the Cygan-Korányi distance
[
9
]
, then by straightforward computations
\phi
does not satisfy (
178
). Hence, we reached a contradiction with (
174
) and the thesis follows.
∎

8.
A representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
on
(-8,Q)

We give a representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
in the strip
(-8,Q)
involving
\mathcal{L}^{3}
.

Proposition 8.1
.

If
\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)
is the Euclidean unit closed ball in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n})\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{n}
is an even
n
-multi-index, then there is a constant
c(n,|\gamma|)>0
such that

(179)

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x^{\gamma}dx=c(n,|\gamma|)\frac{\gamma!}{\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)!},

where by
dx
we denote the integration with respect to the
n
-dimensional Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

The proof follows from
[
2
, Proposition 3.6]
.
Let us recall the map
\pi_{H_{1}}:\mathbb{H}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n},\pi_{H_{1}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n+1})\to(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n}),
and moreover the following proposition holds.

Proposition 8.2
.

For every even
2n
-multi-index
\gamma\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{2n}
and for every
\alpha<Q
the equality

(180)

\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}P_{\alpha}(x)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}=\frac{\frac{\gamma}{2}!}{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}!}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\pi_{H_{1}}(x)^{\gamma}P_{\alpha}(x)d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(x)

holds.

Proof.

Let us recall Lemma
4.1
so that for every even
2n
-multi-index
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{2n})\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{2n}
and every
\alpha<Q
we have

(181)

\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\pi_{H_{1}}(x)^{\gamma}\|x\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{Q-1}(x)=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\pi_{H_{1}}(x)^{\gamma}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}dx

and

(182)

\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}\|x\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}=2\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-|\gamma|}dx.

If for every even
2n
-multi-index
\gamma
and every
\alpha<Q
the relation

(183)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{\alpha-|\gamma|}dx=\frac{\frac{\gamma}{2}!}{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}!}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\pi_{H_{1}}(x)^{\gamma}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}dx

holds, then the thesis follows recalling (
181
) and (
182
).
In order to show (
183
), we recall a consequence of Proposition
8.1
applied to the Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
. In fact, denoting by
\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)
the Euclidean closed unit ball on
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
, and the Euclidean coarea formula, from (
179
) of Proposition
8.1

(184)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,r)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}d\mathcal{H}_{E}^{2n-1}(x)dr=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}d\mathcal{L}^{2n}(x)\\
&=\frac{\frac{\gamma}{2}!}{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}!}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma!}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x^{\gamma}d\mathcal{L}^{2n}(x)=\frac{\frac{\gamma}{2}!}{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}!}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma!}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,r)}x^{\gamma}d\mathcal{H}_{E}^{2n-1}(x)dr\end{split}

hold, where
\mathcal{L}^{2n}
denotes the
2n
-dimensional Lebesgue measure.
Moreover, exploiting the equality (
184
) and the homogeneity of the surface measure
\mathcal{H}^{2n-1}_{E}
with respect to the Euclidean isotropic dilations, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}r^{|\gamma|}dr\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}d\mathcal{H}_{E}^{2n-1}(x)=\frac{\frac{\gamma}{2}!}{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}!}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma!}\int_{0}^{1}r^{|\gamma|}dr\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x^{\gamma}d\mathcal{H}_{E}^{2n-1}(x).

As a consequence, we have that

(185)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}d\mathcal{H}_{E}^{2n-1}(x)=\frac{\frac{\gamma}{2}!}{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}!}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x^{\gamma}d\mathcal{H}_{E}^{2n-1}(x).

Hence, by Fubini’s theorem we can rewrite the left-hand side of (
183
) as

(186)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}dx=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\int_{H_{1}}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}dx_{1}\dots dx_{2n}\right)dx_{2n+1}.

Performing a change of variables on
H_{1}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{2n}
, the same introduced in
[
2
, Section 4]
,

\displaystyle\Omega_{2n}:(0,\infty)\times[0,\pi)^{2n-2}\times[0,2\pi)

\displaystyle\to H_{1}

\displaystyle(\rho,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})

\displaystyle\to\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1}),

with

\displaystyle\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})=\begin{bmatrix}\rho\cos\theta_{1},\\
\rho\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2},\\
\rho\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos\theta_{3},\\
\ldots\\
\rho\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\ldots\sin\theta_{2n-2}\cos\theta_{2n-1},\\
\rho\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\ldots\sin\theta_{2n-2}\sin\theta_{2n-1}\\
\end{bmatrix}^{T},

where the Jacobian of
\Omega_{2n}
is

J\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{2n-1})=\rho^{2n-1}(\sin\theta_{1})^{2n-2}(\sin\theta_{2})^{2n-3}\ldots(\sin\theta_{2n-2}),

from (
186
) it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\int_{H_{1}}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}dx_{1}\dots dx_{2n}\right)dx_{2n+1}

(187)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\pi}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(\rho\cos(\theta_{1}))^{|\gamma|}h(1,(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1}))

\displaystyle\|(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}\rho^{2n-1}(\sin\theta_{1})^{2n-2}(\sin\theta_{2})^{2n-3}\ldots(\sin\theta_{2n-2})d\theta_{1}\dots d\theta_{2n-1}d\rho dx_{2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{|\gamma|+2n-1}\int_{0}^{\pi}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(\cos(\theta_{1}))^{|\gamma|}h(1,(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1}))

\displaystyle\|(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}(\sin\theta_{1})^{2n-2}(\sin\theta_{2})^{2n-3}\ldots(\sin\theta_{2n-2})d\theta_{1}\dots d\theta_{2n-1}d\rho dx_{2n+1}.

We remark now that for every fixed
x_{2n+1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\rho\in(0,\infty)
the function

[0,\pi)^{2n-2}\times[0,2\pi)\ni\theta\to h(1,(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1}))\|(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}

is constant. This is a consequence of the horizontal rotations invariance of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\alpha}
and of the heat kernel
h(1,\cdot)
, since for every fixed
x_{2n+1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\rho\in(0,\infty)
the map

[0,\pi)^{2n-2}\times[0,2\pi)\ni\theta\to(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1})

is a horizontal rotation.
As a consequence, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{|\gamma|+2n-1}\|(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}h(1,(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1}))d\rho dx_{2n+1}\cdot

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\pi}\ldots\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(\cos(\theta_{1}))^{|\gamma|}(\sin\theta_{1})^{2n-2}(\sin\theta_{2})^{2n-3}\ldots(\sin\theta_{2n-2})d\theta_{1}\dots d\theta_{2n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{|\gamma|+2n-1}\|(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1})\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}h(1,(\Omega_{2n}(\rho,\theta),x_{2n+1}))d\rho dx_{2n+1}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{E}(0,1)}x_{1}^{|\gamma|}d\mathcal{H}^{2n-1}_{E}(x).

Repeating the same steps on the right-hand side
\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\pi_{H_{1}}(x)^{\gamma}h(1,x)\|x\|_{c}^{-\alpha-|\gamma|}dx
of (
183
) and exploiting (
185
), we conclude the proof.
∎

,i,j\neq k,\\
[Z_{i},Z_{k}]=[Z_{j},Z_{k}]=0\end{subarray}}^{2n}(Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{i}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle+Z_{j}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{k}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}\phi(x)+Z_{k}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}\phi(x)))\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+3\frac{1}{6!}\frac{1}{\alpha+6}(n-1)T^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x))\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6!}\frac{1}{\alpha+6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1,i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{2n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}}Z_{\sigma}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{i}^{4}y_{j}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha+6}T^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)\left(\frac{1}{4}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2n+1}^{2}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)-\Big(\frac{5}{6!}+\frac{3}{6!}(n-1)\Big)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha+6}\mathcal{L}^{3}\phi(x)\frac{1}{6!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}P_{\alpha}(y)d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y).

Therefore, for
\alpha\in(-8,Q)
we can rewrite (
8
) as

\displaystyle\psi(x,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\phi(xy)P_{\alpha}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}\Big(\phi(xy)-\mathcal{T}_{7}(\phi,x)(xy)\Big)P_{\alpha}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\phi(x)\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{\alpha}\sigma(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)d(\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\frac{1}{4!}\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(x)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

(191)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha+4}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}T^{2}\phi(x)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}dy-\frac{n}{4!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{4}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\bigg)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha+6}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}T^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)\Bigg(\frac{1}{4}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{5}{6!}+\frac{3}{6!}(n-1)\Big)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\Bigg)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha+6}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}^{3}\phi(x)\frac{1}{6!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y).

Finally, in order to establish the representation in (
8
), we are left to prove (
190
). Let us consider then
i,j\in\{1,\dots,2n\},i\neq j
, with
[Z_{i},Z_{j}]\neq 0
. Hence
[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=T
or
[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=-T
. We assume that
[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=T
and we denote by
X=Z_{i}
and
Y=Z_{j}
.
The sum on the left-hand side of (
190
) involves the following terms

(192)

\begin{matrix}XXXXYY\phi(x)&YXXXXY\phi(x)&YXXXYX\phi(x)\\
XXXYXY\phi(x)&XXYXYX\phi(x)&YXXYXX\phi(x)\\
XXYXXY\phi(x)&XYXXYX\phi(x)&XYYXXX\phi(x)\\
XYXXXY\phi(x)&XYXYXX\phi(x)&YXYXXX\phi(x)\\
XXXYYX\phi(x)&XXYYXX\phi(x)&YYXXXX\phi(x)\\
\end{matrix}.

By exploiting the relations among the vector fields
X,Y
and
T
, it can be verified that the sum of the terms of the first column of (
192
) gives

(193)

5X^{4}Y^{2}\phi(x)-8X^{3}YT\phi(x),

the sum of the terms of the third column of (
192
) is

(194)

5Y^{2}X^{4}\phi(x)+8YX^{3}T\phi(x),

and the sum of the terms of the second column of (
192
) equals

(195)

5X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)-X^{3}YT\phi(x)+X^{2}YXT\phi(x)=5X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)-X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x).

We observe also that the following equalities hold

\displaystyle X^{2}T^{2}=XTXT=X(XY-YX)XT=X^{2}YXT-XYX^{2}T

(196)

\displaystyle X^{2}T^{2}=X^{2}(XY-YX)T=X^{3}YT-X^{2}YXT

\displaystyle X^{2}T^{2}=(XY-YX)X^{2}T=XYX^{2}T-YX^{3}T,

hence

3X^{2}T^{2}=X^{3}YT-YX^{3}T.

If we sum up the fifteen terms of (
192
) and we exploit the established relations (
193
), (
194
), (
195
) and (
8
) we get the following result

\displaystyle\ 5(X^{4}Y^{2}\phi(x)+X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)+Y^{2}X^{4}\phi(x))-8X^{3}YT\phi(x)+8YX^{3}T\phi(x)-X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 5(X^{4}Y^{2}\phi(x)+X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)+Y^{2}X^{4}\phi(x))-8(X^{3}YT-YX^{3}T)\phi(x)-X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 5(X^{4}Y^{2}\phi(x)+X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)+Y^{2}X^{4}\phi(x))-8(3X^{2}T^{2})\phi(x)-X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 5(X^{4}Y^{2}\phi(x)+X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)+Y^{2}X^{4}\phi(x))-24X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x)-X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 5(X^{4}Y^{2}\phi(x)+X^{2}Y^{2}X^{2}\phi(x)+Y^{2}X^{4}\phi(x))-25X^{2}T^{2}\phi(x).

One can verify that analogous passages can be carried out if
[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=-T
. Thus, (
190
) is proved.

8.1.
A geometric application, III

Here, we apply the representation obtained in (
8
) in order to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let
h
be the heat kernel associated with
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, then for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,2n\}
the following equalities hold

(197)

\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{i}^{6}h(1,x)dx=5!\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}x_{i}^{2}x_{2n+1}^{2}h(1,x)dx=\frac{2}{3}(5+3(n-1)).

Proof.

As a consequence of the representation of
\psi(x,\alpha)
in (
8
) valid for
\alpha\in(-8,Q)
, exploiting Lemma
4.1
and taking into account the definition (
142
), we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{3}\phi(x)=\lim_{\alpha\to-6}\psi(x,\alpha)

(198)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to-6}\Bigg(-\frac{1}{\alpha+6}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}T^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)\Bigg(\frac{1}{4}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{2}y_{2n+1}^{2}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{5}{6!}+\frac{3}{6!}(n-1)\Big)\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\Bigg)-\frac{1}{\alpha+6}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}\mathcal{L}^{3}\phi(x)\frac{1}{6!}\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}_{c}(0,1)}y_{1}^{6}\|y\|_{\alpha}^{\alpha-Q}d\mathcal{S}^{Q-1}_{\infty}(y)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 6T^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)\left(\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{2}y_{2n+1}^{2}h(1,y)dy-\Big(\frac{5}{6!}+\frac{3}{6!}(n-1)\Big)\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{6}h(1,y)dy\right)

\displaystyle+6\mathcal{L}^{3}\phi(x)\frac{1}{6!}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}y_{1}^{6}h(1,y)dy.

If we show that there is not any constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that the equality

(199)

\mathcal{L}^{3}\phi(x)=cT^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x)

holds for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
, then the proof follows from (
8.1
). Let us assume by contradiction that (
199
) is true, then the following equality holds

(200)

\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{L}\phi)(x)=cT^{2}\mathcal{L}\phi(x),

for every
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
.
According to our reasoning, it would exist a constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(201)

\mathcal{L}^{2}\phi(x)=cT^{2}\phi(x),

for every
\phi=\mathcal{L}(\psi)
for some
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
and for every
x\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
. Then, by following the same reasoning adopted in the proof of Proposition
7.3
, in order to show a contradiction with (
201
) it is sufficient to individuate a function
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
such that

(202)

\Delta^{2}(\mathcal{L}\psi)(0)\neq 0.

For example
\psi(x)=x_{1}^{6}e^{-\|x\|_{K}^{4}}
satisfies (
202
).
Thus, (
201
) is not true, hence we reach a contradiction with (
199
) and the thesis follows.
∎
